1	import { useMemo, useState } from "react";
2	
3	import { InteractiveCard } from "../NoteInteractions";
4	import { cn } from "@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/lib/cn";
5	
6	type IndicatorKind = "premise" | "conclusion";
7	
8	type IndicatorItem = {
9	  word: string;
10	  kind: IndicatorKind;
11	  note: string;
12	};
13	
14	const ITEMS: IndicatorItem[] = [
15	  { word: "Therefore", kind: "conclusion", note: "Points forward to the claim the author is proving." },
16	  { word: "Because", kind: "premise", note: "Hands you a reason; the support sits right after it." },
17	  { word: "Thus", kind: "conclusion", note: "Wraps up the reasoning into the author's point." },
18	  { word: "Since", kind: "premise", note: "Usually drops a fact first, then leans on it." },
19	  { word: "For", kind: "premise", note: "A quiet FABS word: the evidence follows it." },
20	  { word: "So", kind: "conclusion", note: "Signals the payoff the premises were building to." },
21	  { word: "After all", kind: "premise", note: "Another FABS reason offered to back the claim." },
22	  { word: "Hence", kind: "conclusion", note: "Formal cousin of “therefore”: the conclusion lands here." },
23	];
24	
25	const OPTIONS: { value: IndicatorKind; label: string }[] = [
26	  { value: "premise", label: "Premise indicator" },
27	  { value: "conclusion", label: "Conclusion indicator" },
28	];
29	
30	export function IndicatorPopQuiz() {
31	  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);
32	  const [answers, setAnswers] = useState<Record<string, IndicatorKind>>({});
33	
34	  const item = ITEMS[index];
35	  const selected = answers[item.word] ?? null;
36	  const answered = selected !== null;
37	  const isCorrect = answered && selected === item.kind;
38	  const score = useMemo(
39	    () => ITEMS.filter((entry) => answers[entry.word] === entry.kind).length,
40	    [answers],
41	  );
42	  const completed = Object.keys(answers).length === ITEMS.length;
43	
44	  function pick(value: IndicatorKind) {
45	    if (answered) return;
46	    setAnswers((current) => ({ ...current, [item.word]: value }));
47	  }
48	
49	  return (
50	    <InteractiveCard
51	      prominent
52	      className="border-[#D8BE82] bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,#FFFDF8_0%,#FBF4E7_100%)] p-6 shadow-[0_24px_54px_rgba(92,67,20,0.11)] md:p-8"
53	    >
54	      <div className="space-y-6">
55	        <div className="flex flex-wrap items-end justify-between gap-3">
56	          <div className="space-y-2">
57	            <div className="inline-flex rounded-full border border-[#E6D6B2] bg-white/70 px-3 py-1 text-[11px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-[#74541B]">
58	              Pop quiz
59	            </div>
60	            <h3 className="font-serif text-[30px] italic leading-tight text-[#1a1a1a]">
61	              Premise or conclusion indicator?
62	            </h3>
63	            <p className="max-w-2xl text-[16px] leading-[1.6] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.74)]">
64	              One word at a time. Tag what each indicator is pointing at.
65	            </p>
66	          </div>
67	          <div className="text-[12px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-[rgba(116,84,27,0.7)]">
68	            {score}/{ITEMS.length} right
69	          </div>
70	        </div>
71	
72	        <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-1.5" aria-hidden="true">
73	          {ITEMS.map((entry, entryIndex) => {
74	            const entryAnswer = answers[entry.word];
75	            const state = !entryAnswer
76	              ? "bg-[#E6D6B2]"
77	              : entryAnswer === entry.kind
78	                ? "bg-[#2F5B35]"
79	                : "bg-[#B4763E]";
80	            return (
81	              <span
82	                key={entry.word}
83	                className={cn(
84	                  "h-1.5 rounded-full transition-all duration-200",
85	                  entryIndex === index ? "w-6" : "w-3",
86	                  state,
87	                )}
88	              />
89	            );
90	          })}
91	        </div>
92	
93	        <div className="rounded-[18px] border border-[rgba(116,84,27,0.18)] bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,#FFFFFF_0%,#FFF9EF_100%)] px-6 py-8 text-center shadow-[inset_0_1px_0_rgba(255,255,255,0.9)]">
94	          <p className="text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.22em] text-[rgba(116,84,27,0.6)]">
95	            Indicator {index + 1} of {ITEMS.length}
96	          </p>
97	          <p className="mt-3 font-serif text-[40px] italic leading-tight text-[#1a1a1a] md:text-[48px]">
98	            {item.word}
99	          </p>
100	        </div>
101	
102	        <div className="grid gap-3 sm:grid-cols-2">
103	          {OPTIONS.map((option) => {
104	            const isPicked = selected === option.value;
105	            const isAnswerKey = answered && option.value === item.kind;
106	            return (
107	              <button
108	                key={option.value}
109	                type="button"
110	                onClick={() => pick(option.value)}
111	                disabled={answered}
112	                aria-pressed={isPicked}
113	                className={cn(
114	                  "rounded-[14px] border px-5 py-4 text-[16px] font-semibold transition-[border-color,background,box-shadow,transform] duration-200 ease-out",
115	                  "focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-[#D5AD5F] focus-visible:ring-offset-2",
116	                  !answered &&
117	                    "border-[#E6D6B2] bg-white/90 text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.78)] shadow-[0_8px_18px_rgba(92,67,20,0.05)] hover:-translate-y-[1px] hover:border-[#D5AD5F] hover:bg-[#FFF8EA] hover:shadow-[0_16px_30px_rgba(92,67,20,0.08)]",
118	                  isAnswerKey && "border-[#B9D6B4] bg-[#F6FBF3] text-[#2F5B35]",
119	                  answered && isPicked && !isCorrect && "border-[#E0B48A] bg-[#FBEFE3] text-[#8A4B1F]",
120	                  answered && !isPicked && !isAnswerKey && "border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.08)] bg-white/55 text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.5)]",
121	                )}
122	              >
123	                {option.label}
124	              </button>
125	            );
126	          })}
127	        </div>
128	
129	        {answered ? (
130	          <div
131	            role="status"
132	            aria-live="polite"
133	            className={cn(
134	              "rounded-[14px] border px-5 py-4 text-[15px] leading-[1.6] shadow-[0_12px_24px_rgba(26,26,26,0.04)]",
135	              isCorrect ? "border-[#B9D6B4] bg-[#F6FBF3] text-[#2F5B35]" : "border-[#E0B48A] bg-[#FBEFE3] text-[#7A451C]",
136	            )}
137	          >
138	            <p>
139	              <strong>
140	                {isCorrect ? "Right." : "Not quite."}
141	              </strong>{" "}
142	              <span className="text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.78)]">
143	                “{item.word}” is a {item.kind} indicator. {item.note}
144	              </span>
145	            </p>
146	            {!completed ? (
147	              <button
148	                type="button"
149	                onClick={() => setIndex((current) => Math.min(current + 1, ITEMS.length - 1))}
150	                className="mt-4 rounded-full border border-[#1a1a1a] bg-[#1a1a1a] px-5 py-2 text-[12px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.12em] text-[#F5F2ED] transition-[background,transform] duration-200 hover:-translate-y-[1px] hover:bg-[rgba(26,26,26,0.88)] focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-[#D5AD5F] focus-visible:ring-offset-2"
151	              >
152	                Next word
153	              </button>
154	            ) : (
155	              <p className="mt-3 text-[13px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.14em] text-[rgba(116,84,27,0.72)]">
156	                Done — {score}/{ITEMS.length} indicators tagged.
157	              </p>
158	            )}
159	          </div>
160	        ) : null}
161	      </div>
162	    </InteractiveCard>
163	  );
164	}

75	export default function ArgumentBasicsNotes() {
76	  return (
77	    <IntroNotesShell
78	      eyebrow="Lesson 1"
79	      title="Stimulus First."
80	      subtitle="Read the passage before you worry about what the question or answers want from you."
81	    >
82	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Primary Directive" className="space-y-8">
83	        <SectionHeading title="Primary Directive" subtitle="Start with the passage itself" />
84	        <p className="font-serif text-[30px] italic leading-[1.35] text-[#1a1a1a]">
85	          The stimulus is the passage. It is the source of the work. Read it first, read it closely, and figure out what it is saying before you move anywhere else.
86	        </p>
87	        <CalloutCard>
88	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[#1a1a1a]">
89	            Your first question is not, &quot;Which answer looks good?&quot; Your first question is, &quot;What does this stimulus actually say?&quot;
90	          </p>
91	        </CalloutCard>
92	      </LessonSection>
93	
94	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Question Parts" className="space-y-8">
95	        <SectionHeading title="Question Parts" subtitle="The three parts of every LR question" />
96	        <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.84)]">
97	          Every Logical Reasoning question has the same three visible pieces. They do different jobs, and the stimulus comes first because it gives you the material the rest of the question is testing.
98	        </p>
99	        <div className="space-y-4">
100	          <RowCard
101	            left="1. Stimulus"
102	            right="The short passage. It may be an argument, a set of facts, a debate, or a description. Your first job is simply to understand what it says."
103	          />
104	          <RowCard
105	            left="2. Question Stem"
106	            right="The stem tells you what task to perform with the stimulus after you understand it."
107	          />
108	          <RowCard
109	            left="3. Answer Choices"
110	            right="The choices test whether you understood the stimulus and can do the task the stem asks for. They do not replace close reading."
111	          />
112	        </div>
113	      </LessonSection>
114	
115	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Reading Style" className="space-y-8">
116	        <SectionHeading title="Reading Style" subtitle="Passive reading vs. active reading" />
117	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
118	          <GridCard title="Passive Reading">
119	            <p>Eyes move across the words, but the structure stays blurry.</p>
120	            <p>You finish the passage and only have a vague impression of what happened.</p>
121	            <p>That is when answer choices start to feel like guesses.</p>
122	          </GridCard>
123	          <GridCard title="Active Reading">
124	            <p>You track what each sentence is contributing.</p>
125	            <p>You notice whether the passage is just information or whether it is trying to persuade you of something.</p>
126	            <p>You reach the stem already knowing what the stimulus said.</p>
127	          </GridCard>
128	        </div>
129	        <CalloutCard className="bg-white">
130	          <Kicker>Student job</Kicker>
131	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.84)]">
132	            Stay with the passage long enough to classify it: is this merely information, or is this an argument?
133	          </p>
134	        </CalloutCard>
135	      </LessonSection>
136	
137	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Stimulus Example" className="space-y-8">
138	        <SectionHeading title="Stimulus Example" subtitle="Ask what the passage is saying" />
139	        <CalloutCard className="bg-[#FCFAF5]">
140	          <p className="font-serif text-[22px] italic leading-[1.7] text-[#1a1a1a]">
141	            &quot;Last year, the city planted hundreds of new trees along its busiest streets. This spring, residents in those neighborhoods reported lower afternoon temperatures than they had the previous year.&quot;
142	          </p>
143	        </CalloutCard>
144	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
145	          <GridCard title="What to notice first">
146	            <p>The passage is giving you information about two events: tree planting and cooler neighborhood temperatures.</p>
147	            <p>Your immediate task is to understand that relationship before doing anything else.</p>
148	          </GridCard>
149	          <GridCard title="What not to do yet">
150	            <p>Do not jump ahead to flaws, assumptions, or answer-choice comparisons.</p>
151	            <p>First say, in plain language, what the stimulus is telling you.</p>
152	          </GridCard>
153	        </div>
154	      </LessonSection>
155	
156	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Student Takeaway" className="space-y-8 border-b-0">
157	        <SectionHeading title="Student Takeaway" subtitle="The first-page rule" />
158	        <CalloutCard className="border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.92)] bg-[#1a1a1a] text-[#F5F2ED]">
159	          <p className="text-[22px] leading-[1.8]">
160	            The stimulus is the passage; read it first; identify whether it is merely information or an argument.
161	          </p>
162	          <p className="mt-6 text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[rgba(245,242,237,0.84)]">
163	            That is enough for page one. Once you can do that reliably, the later lessons can teach you how arguments are built.
164	          </p>
165	        </CalloutCard>
166	      </LessonSection>
167	    </IntroNotesShell>
168	  );
169	}

62	export default function WhereConclusionsHideNotes() {
63	  return (
64	    <IntroNotesShell
65	      eyebrow="Lesson 3"
66	      title="Where Conclusions Hide."
67	      subtitle="Once you know what a conclusion is, learn the places authors like to tuck it."
68	    >
69	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Placement rule" className="space-y-8">
70	        <SectionHeading title="Placement rule" subtitle="Location gives you a candidate" />
71	        <p className="font-serif text-[30px] italic leading-[1.35] text-[#1a1a1a]">
72	          The conclusion is not always last. Its location can help you spot it, but placement only gives you a candidate.
73	        </p>
74	        <CalloutCard>
75	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[#1a1a1a]">
76	            By this point you already know indicators and the 2-part check. Use those tools here instead of relearning them.
77	          </p>
78	        </CalloutCard>
79	      </LessonSection>
80	
81	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Common hiding spots" className="space-y-8">
82	        <SectionHeading title="Common hiding spots" subtitle="The places to check first" />
83	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
84	          <GridCard title="First sentence">
85	            <p>The passage opens with a judgment, then spends the remaining lines defending it.</p>
86	          </GridCard>
87	          <GridCard title="After a pivot">
88	            <p>A sentence after a turn such as &quot;but&quot; or &quot;however&quot; often contains the author&apos;s real point.</p>
89	          </GridCard>
90	          <GridCard title="Between setup and evidence">
91	            <p>The author may give background, state the point, then support it. The middle sentence can be the target.</p>
92	          </GridCard>
93	          <GridCard title="After rebuttal context">
94	            <p>Once an opposing view or concession appears, the author&apos;s response often carries the conclusion.</p>
95	          </GridCard>
96	        </div>
97	      </LessonSection>
98	
99	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Pattern example" className="space-y-8">
100	        <SectionHeading title="Pattern example" subtitle="Watch the placement, not just the wording" />
101	        <CalloutCard className="bg-[#FCFAF5]">
102	          <p className="font-serif text-[22px] italic leading-[1.7] text-[#1a1a1a]">
103	            &quot;Many critics say the museum expansion is too expensive. However, the project should proceed because private donors have already covered most of the cost and the additional galleries will double student access to the collection.&quot;
104	          </p>
105	        </CalloutCard>
106	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
107	          <GridCard title="What comes first">
108	            <p>The opening reports someone else&apos;s criticism.</p>
109	            <p>That sets up contrast, not the author&apos;s final point.</p>
110	          </GridCard>
111	          <GridCard title="Where the conclusion lands">
112	            <p>After the pivot, the author states the real claim: the project should proceed.</p>
113	            <p>The remaining material supports that claim.</p>
114	          </GridCard>
115	        </div>
116	      </LessonSection>
117	
118	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Student takeaway" className="space-y-8 border-b-0">
119	        <SectionHeading title="Student takeaway" subtitle="Placement is a search strategy" />
120	        <CalloutCard className="border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.92)] bg-[#1a1a1a] text-[#F5F2ED]">
121	          <p className="text-[22px] leading-[1.8]">
122	            Check the usual hiding spots first, then confirm the candidate with support. Location helps you find the conclusion; structure proves you found the right one.
123	          </p>
124	        </CalloutCard>
125	      </LessonSection>
126	    </IntroNotesShell>
127	  );
128	}

75	export default function LayeredArgumentsNotes() {
76	  return (
77	    <IntroNotesShell
78	      eyebrow="Lesson 4"
79	      title="Layered Arguments."
80	      subtitle="Some claims get support and then turn around and support something larger."
81	    >
82	      <LessonSection tocTitle="The chain" className="space-y-8">
83	        <SectionHeading title="The chain" subtitle="When one conclusion becomes a premise" />
84	        <p className="font-serif text-[30px] italic leading-[1.35] text-[#1a1a1a]">
85	          A layered argument has a middle claim that does double duty: it is supported by earlier reasons, then used to support the main conclusion.
86	        </p>
87	      </LessonSection>
88	
89	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Bridge test" className="space-y-8">
90	        <SectionHeading title="Bridge test" subtitle="How to spot the intermediate conclusion" />
91	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
92	          <GridCard title="Check 1">
93	            <p>What supports this claim?</p>
94	          </GridCard>
95	          <GridCard title="Check 2">
96	            <p>What does this claim support?</p>
97	          </GridCard>
98	        </div>
99	        <CalloutCard>
100	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[#1a1a1a]">
101	            If the answer to both questions is &quot;something in the stimulus,&quot; you are probably looking at an intermediate conclusion.
102	          </p>
103	        </CalloutCard>
104	      </LessonSection>
105	
106	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Supported vs not fully supported" className="space-y-8">
107	        <SectionHeading title="Supported vs not fully supported" subtitle="Why some chains still leave a gap" />
108	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
109	          <GridCard title="Supported">
110	            <p>Each step in the chain gives enough reason to move to the next claim.</p>
111	          </GridCard>
112	          <GridCard title="Not fully supported">
113	            <p>The argument makes a jump. The premises or intermediate conclusion suggest the next step, but do not fully earn it.</p>
114	          </GridCard>
115	        </div>
116	      </LessonSection>
117	
118	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Gap example" className="space-y-8">
119	        <SectionHeading title="Gap example" subtitle="Later-stage validity language" />
120	        <CalloutCard className="bg-[#FCFAF5]">
121	          <p className="font-serif text-[22px] italic leading-[1.7] text-[#1a1a1a]">
122	            &quot;The school&apos;s debate team has won every regional meet this year, so it is clearly the best team in the country. Therefore, the school should cut funding for all other extracurricular programs and focus almost entirely on debate.&quot;
123	          </p>
124	        </CalloutCard>
125	        <div className="space-y-4">
126	          <RowCard
127	            left="Intermediate conclusion"
128	            right="The team is the best in the country."
129	          />
130	          <RowCard
131	            left="Main conclusion"
132	            right="The school should focus almost entirely on debate."
133	          />
134	          <RowCard
135	            left="Why the chain fails"
136	            right="Even if the first step were strong, the argument still jumps from debate success to a sweeping funding decision."
137	          />
138	        </div>
139	        <CalloutCard className="bg-white">
140	          <Kicker>Later-language note</Kicker>
141	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.84)]">
142	            In stricter logic terms, a claim is valid only when the support guarantees it. On the LSAT, many arguments feel persuasive without reaching that standard, which is why beginner pages first talk about support versus not fully supported.
143	          </p>
144	        </CalloutCard>
145	      </LessonSection>
146	
147	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Student takeaway" className="space-y-8 border-b-0">
148	        <SectionHeading title="Student takeaway" subtitle="Map each step before you judge it" />
149	        <CalloutCard className="border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.92)] bg-[#1a1a1a] text-[#F5F2ED]">
150	          <p className="text-[22px] leading-[1.8]">
151	            In layered arguments, identify the middle claim first, then ask whether each step is genuinely supported or only loosely suggested.
152	          </p>
153	        </CalloutCard>
154	      </LessonSection>
155	    </IntroNotesShell>
156	  );
157	}

1	/**
2	 * Phased lesson content — opt-in. Lessons listed here render the PhaseRunner
3	 * (briefing → demo → attempt → reveal → coach → checkpoint → next).
4	 * Lessons NOT in this map fall back to the legacy prose-block renderer.
5	 *
6	 * Add a new lesson by mapping its `number` (e.g. "1.2") to a PhasedLesson.
7	 *
8	 * Source-anchored to lessons.generated.json[1.1] (Monica claimed dinosaurs).
9	 */
10	
11	export type Role = 'conclusion' | 'premise' | 'opposing' | 'background';
12	
13	/** Six categories of LSAT indicator words (M1.3 + Indicator Vault). */
14	export type IndicatorCategory =
15	  | 'conclusion'
16	  | 'premise'
17	  | 'pivot'
18	  | 'opinion'
19	  | 'opposing'
20	  | 'concession';
21	
22	export interface RoleLabelTask {
23	  kind: 'role-label';
24	  /** Stimulus split into segments that the student labels. */
25	  segments: Array<{ id: string; text: string; correct: Role }>;
26	  /** Roles the student is allowed to pick from for this task. */
27	  allowedRoles: Role[];
28	  /** Per-segment teaching note shown after reveal. */
29	  rationale: Record<string, string>;
30	}
31	
32	export interface IndicatorTagTask {
33	  kind: 'indicator-tag';
34	  /** Sentence shown to the student with one or more words to tag. */
35	  sentence: string;
36	  /** The substrings to tag, with their correct category. */
37	  targets: Array<{
38	    id: string;
39	    /** Exact substring to highlight (case-sensitive, single occurrence). */
40	    word: string;
41	    correct: IndicatorCategory;
42	    /** Per-target teaching note shown after reveal. */
43	    rationale: string;
44	  }>;
45	  /** Categories the student picks from. */
46	  allowedCategories: IndicatorCategory[];
47	}
48	
49	export interface ConclusionPickTask {
50	  kind: 'conclusion-pick';
51	  /** Stimulus shown as one block to the student. */
52	  stimulus: string;
53	  /** Each candidate is a sentence the student could pick as the main conclusion. */
54	  candidates: Array<{
55	    id: string;
56	    text: string;
57	    is_main: boolean;
58	    /** Why this is or isn't the main conclusion. */
59	    rationale: string;
60	  }>;
61	}
62	
63	export type AttemptTask = RoleLabelTask | IndicatorTagTask | ConclusionPickTask;
64	
65	export interface PhaseBriefing {
66	  kind: 'briefing';
67	  /** One-line "what you'll do here". Verb-led. */
68	  goal: string;
69	  /** Two short sentences max. Plain language, no spec voice. */
70	  body: string;
71	  /** Word the student should leave the briefing with — surfaces in the demo. */
72	  primer?: string;
73	}
74	
75	export interface PhaseDemo {
76	  kind: 'demo';
77	  title: string;
78	  /** Walk-through copy. Short. */
79	  body: string;
80	  /** Optional pre-labeled stimulus to *show* what role-labeling looks like. */
81	  exampleSegments?: Array<{ text: string; role: Role; whisper?: string }>;
82	  /** Optional interactive widget rendered after `body`. Demos with an
83	   *  interactive widget should usually omit `exampleSegments` to keep the
84	   *  surface focused. */
85	  interactive?:
86	    | {
87	        kind: 'pronoun-unpack';
88	        prompt?: string;
89	        segments: Array<
90	          | { kind: 'text'; text: string }
91	          | { kind: 'pronoun'; spanId: string }
92	        >;
93	        spans: Array<{ id: string; pronoun: string; antecedent: string }>;
94	        caption?: string;
95	      }
96	    | {
97	        kind: 'absolute-statements';
98	        caption?: string;
99	        scenarios: Array<{
100	          id: string;
101	          statement: string;
102	          explanation: string;
103	          invalidation: string;
104	          softened: string;
105	          softWord: string;
106	          softReason: string;
107	        }>;
108	      };
109	}
110	
111	export interface PhaseAttempt {
112	  kind: 'attempt';
113	  title: string;
114	  prompt: string;
115	  task: AttemptTask;
116	}
117	
118	export interface PhaseReveal {
119	  kind: 'reveal';
120	  title: string;
121	  /** One-paragraph wrap-up referring back to the student's attempt. */
122	  body: string;
123	}
124	
125	export interface PhaseCoach {
126	  kind: 'coach';
127	  title: string;
128	  /** Three short sections — same shape as CoachNoteCard but lesson-scoped. */
129	  structure_map: string;
130	  core_move: string;
131	  why_it_matters: string;
132	}
133	
134	export interface PhaseCheckpoint {
135	  kind: 'checkpoint';
136	  prompt: string;
137	  options: Array<{ id: string; text: string; correct: boolean; reveal: string }>;
138	}
139	
140	export type Phase =
141	  | PhaseBriefing
142	  | PhaseDemo
143	  | PhaseAttempt
144	  | PhaseReveal
145	  | PhaseCoach
146	  | PhaseCheckpoint;
147	
148	export interface PhasedLesson {
149	  number: string;
150	  /** Student-facing eyebrow ("Lesson 1") — no internal IDs. */
151	  studentEyebrow: string;
152	  title: string;
153	  hook: string;
154	  phases: Phase[];
155	}
156

195	const ROLE_LABELER_ROLES: RoleLabelerRole[] = [
196	  { id: "background", label: "Background", tone: "background" },
197	  { id: "opposing-view", label: "Opposing View", tone: "opposing" },
198	  { id: "concession", label: "Concession", tone: "concession" },
199	  { id: "pivot", label: "Pivot", tone: "pivot" },
200	  { id: "author-premise", label: "Author's Premise", tone: "premise" },
201	  { id: "author-conclusion", label: "Author's Conclusion", tone: "conclusion" },

204	const BeyondPremisesAndConclusions = () => {
205	  const [hoveredRoleTitle, setHoveredRoleTitle] = useState<string | null>(null);
206	  const reducedMotion = useReducedMotion();
207	
208	  return (
209	    <>
210	      <LessonSection tocTitle="The Mindset Shift" className="space-y-16">
211	        <motion.div 
212	          initial={{ opacity: 0, x: -10 }}
213	          whileInView={{ opacity: 1, x: 0 }}
214	          viewport={{ once: true }}
215	          className="relative py-12 px-8 border-l-2 border-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.1)] bg-[oklch(0.98_0.005_60)] rounded-r-2xl"
216	        >
217	          <p className="font-serif text-[32px] md:text-[40px] italic leading-[1.1] text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)] tracking-tight">
218	            Do not ask what the sentence says. <br />
219	            Ask <span className="text-[oklch(0.65_0.12_85)] underline decoration-[oklch(0.65_0.12_85/0.3)] underline-offset-8">what job</span> it is doing.
220	          </p>
221	          <div className="absolute top-0 right-0 p-6 opacity-[0.03] pointer-events-none">
222	            <div className="text-[120px] font-serif italic leading-none">!</div>
223	          </div>
224	        </motion.div>
225	
226	        <div className="space-y-6 text-body-prose text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.76)] max-w-[64ch]">
227	          <p className="text-[20px] font-medium text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)] leading-snug">
228	            A sentence may sound important and still not be the author's conclusion.
229	          </p>
230	          <p>The role matters more than the topic. Your job is to separate the author's argument from everything around it.</p>
231	        </div>
232	      </LessonSection>
233	
234	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Role Guide" className="space-y-16">
235	        <SectionHeading title="Role Guide" subtitle="A compact guide to Background, Opposing Viewpoint, Concession, and Pivot." />
236	        {[
237	          {
238	            title: "Background",
239	            subtitle: "Context setting",
240	            definition: "Sets the stage. It tells you what the argument is about, but it is not automatically evidence for the author's conclusion.",
241	            example: "Several cities have recently expanded their bike-lane networks.",
242	            read: "Do not hunt for a keyword here. Ask whether the sentence is setting the scene or proving the author’s point.",
243	            trap: "Treating relevant context as support. Relevance is not enough. A premise has to support the author's conclusion."
244	          },
245	          {
246	            title: "Opposing Viewpoint",
247	            subtitle: "Other People's Views",
248	            definition: "Someone else's view, often included so the author can respond to it. Sometimes the stimulus gives reasons for someone else’s view. Treat that as part of the opposing-view setup. Ask whose point the sentence is supporting.",
249	            example: "Critics argue that the new transit plan is too expensive.",
250	            read: "Do not hunt for a keyword here. Ask whose view the sentence belongs to.",
251	            trap: "Mistaking another person's view for the author's conclusion."
252	          },
253	          {
254	            title: "Concession",
255	            subtitle: "What the author gives away",
256	            definition: "Something the author admits, grants, or allows before making the real argument.",
257	            example: "Although vegetarian diets require careful planning, they can still provide complete nutrition.",
258	            read: "Useful indicators: although, while, despite, granted, admittedly, though it is true, yes, it is true that, naturally.",
259	            trap: "Mistaking a concession for the author's main point because it sounds important or balanced."
260	          },
261	          {
262	            title: "Pivot",
263	            subtitle: "The turn to the author's position",
264	            definition: "The turn in the argument, showing the move away from other material toward the author's actual position.",
265	            example: "Many residents oppose the plan. However, the city should move forward.",
266	            read: "Useful indicators: but, yet, however, nevertheless, nonetheless, on the other hand.",
267	            trap: "Ignoring pivot words and accidentally choosing the opposing view or concession as the conclusion."
268	          }
269	        ].map((role, i) => (
270	          <motion.div 
271	            key={role.title} 
272	            initial={{ opacity: 0, y: 12 }}
273	            whileInView={{ opacity: 1, y: 0 }}
274	            viewport={{ once: true }}
275	            className="space-y-10"
276	          >
277	            <div className="flex items-baseline gap-4">
278	              <h3 className="font-serif text-[32px] italic text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)] tracking-tight">{role.title}</h3>
279	              <span className="font-sans text-[11px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.4)]">{role.subtitle}</span>
280	            </div>
281	            <div className="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-12">
282	              <div className="space-y-10">
283	                <div>
284	                  <h4 className="text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.4)] mb-4">What it is</h4>
285	                  <p className="text-[17px] leading-relaxed text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.76)]">{role.definition}</p>
286	                </div>
287	                <div>
288	                  <h4 className="text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.4)] mb-4">How to read it</h4>
289	                  {role.read.startsWith("Useful indicators:") ? (
290	                    <div className="space-y-4 pt-1">
291	                      <p className="text-[12px] font-bold uppercase tracking-wider text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.3)]">Useful indicators:</p>
292	                      <div className="flex flex-wrap gap-x-2 gap-y-3">
293	                        {role.read.replace("Useful indicators: ", "").split(", ").map(word => (
294	                          <motion.span 
295	                            key={word}
296	                            onMouseEnter={() => setHoveredRoleTitle(role.title)}
297	                            onMouseLeave={() => setHoveredRoleTitle(null)}
298	                            animate={!reducedMotion && hoveredRoleTitle === role.title ? {
299	                              scale: [1, 1.05, 1],
300	                              transition: { duration: 1.2, repeat: Infinity, ease: "easeInOut" }
301	                            } : { scale: 1 }}
302	                            className={cn(
303	                              "mc-shine px-3 py-1.5 rounded-lg text-[11.5px] font-mono tracking-wider border shadow-[0_1px_2px_rgba(0,0,0,0.02)] transition-all cursor-default select-none bg-white",
304	                              role.title === "Concession" && "border-role-concession/30 text-[oklch(0.4_0.15_85)] hover:border-role-concession/50 hover:shadow-md",
305	                              role.title === "Pivot" && "border-role-pivot/30 text-[oklch(0.4_0.15_45)] hover:border-role-pivot/50 hover:shadow-md",
306	                              role.title !== "Concession" && role.title !== "Pivot" && "border-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.08)] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.8)] hover:border-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.15)]"
307	                            )}
308	                          >
309	                            {word}
310	                          </motion.span>
311	                        ))}
312	                      </div>
313	                    </div>
314	                  ) : (
315	                    <p className="text-[17px] leading-relaxed text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.76)]">{role.read}</p>
316	                  )}
317	                </div>
318	              </div>
319	              <div className="space-y-8">
320	                <div className="rounded-[20px] border border-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.08)] bg-[oklch(0.98_0.005_60)] p-8 shadow-sm">
321	                  <h4 className="text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-[oklch(0.65_0.12_85)] mb-4">Example</h4>
322	                  <p className="text-[20px] leading-relaxed text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)] font-serif italic">{`"${role.example}"`}</p>
323	                </div>
324	                <div className="rounded-[20px] border border-mc-error/10 bg-mc-error/5 p-8">
325	                  <h4 className="text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-mc-error/60 mb-4">Common trap</h4>
326	                  <p className="text-[16px] leading-relaxed text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.76)]">{role.trap}</p>
327	                </div>
328	              </div>
329	            </div>
330	            {i < 3 && <div className="h-px bg-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.06)] pt-12" />}
331	          </motion.div>
332	        ))}
333	      </LessonSection>
334	      
335	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Final Summary" className="space-y-16 border-b-0 pb-20">
336	        <SectionHeading title="Final Summary" subtitle="Keep the core rule in mind" />
337	        <div className="space-y-6 text-body-prose text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.76)] max-w-[64ch]">
338	          <p>Mastering LR requires more than just spotting the conclusion. It requires active reading to identify the function of every sentence.</p>
339	          <p>Background, Opposing Viewpoint, Concession, and Pivot are all common structures that distract from the main point.</p>
340	          <p>By labeling these roles, you keep the argument itself clear and shielded from distractions.</p>
341	        </div>
342	      </LessonSection>
343	    </>
344	  );
345	};

83	export default function ArgumentAnatomyNotes() {
84	  return (
85	    <IntroNotesShell
86	      eyebrow="Lesson 2"
87	      title="Argument Anatomy."
88	      subtitle="Now that you can read the stimulus first, learn how to tell when it is making a case."
89	    >
90	      <LessonSection tocTitle="What an argument is" className="space-y-8">
91	        <SectionHeading title="What an argument is" subtitle="A claim supported by reasons" />
92	        <p className="font-serif text-[30px] italic leading-[1.35] text-[#1a1a1a]">
93	          An argument is not just a topic. It is a claim the author wants you to accept, backed by reasons the author offers in support.
94	        </p>
95	        <CalloutCard>
96	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[#1a1a1a]">
97	            When a stimulus gives reasons for a point, you are looking at argument structure. When it merely reports facts, you are not there yet.
98	          </p>
99	        </CalloutCard>
100	      </LessonSection>
101	
102	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Not every stimulus" className="space-y-8">
103	        <SectionHeading title="Not every stimulus" subtitle="Some passages inform; some argue" />
104	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
105	          <GridCard title="Mere information">
106	            <p>The stimulus reports facts, events, or descriptions.</p>
107	            <p>No sentence is being offered as a reason to accept a further claim.</p>
108	          </GridCard>
109	          <GridCard title="Argument">
110	            <p>At least one claim is doing support work for another claim.</p>
111	            <p>The author is moving from reasons toward a point.</p>
112	          </GridCard>
113	        </div>
114	      </LessonSection>
115	
116	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Premises and conclusions" className="space-y-8">
117	        <SectionHeading title="Premises and conclusions" subtitle="Support and claim" />
118	        <div className="grid gap-5 md:grid-cols-2">
119	          <GridCard title="Premises ARE">
120	            <RuleList items={["Facts", "Evidence", "Reasons", "Support for another claim"]} />
121	          </GridCard>
122	          <GridCard title="Premises ARE NOT">
123	            <RuleList items={["The author's final point", "The claim being proven", "The statement everything else points toward"]} />
124	          </GridCard>
125	          <GridCard title="Conclusions ARE">
126	            <RuleList items={["The author's claim", "The point argued for", "The sentence supported by other claims"]} />
127	          </GridCard>
128	          <GridCard title="Conclusions ARE NOT">
129	            <RuleList items={["Random background detail", "Automatically true just because it appears", "A sentence with no support relationship"]} />
130	          </GridCard>
131	        </div>
132	      </LessonSection>
133	
134	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Anatomy example" className="space-y-8">
135	        <SectionHeading title="Anatomy example" subtitle="Label the parts of a simple argument" />
136	        <CalloutCard className="bg-[#FCFAF5]">
137	          <p className="font-serif text-[22px] italic leading-[1.7] text-[#1a1a1a]">
138	            &quot;The city should extend library hours, because evening attendance has increased steadily for three years and nearby branches with longer hours serve more students after school.&quot;
139	          </p>
140	        </CalloutCard>
141	        <div className="space-y-4">
142	          <RowCard
143	            left="Conclusion"
144	            right="The city should extend library hours."
145	          />
146	          <RowCard
147	            left="Premise 1"
148	            right="Evening attendance has increased steadily for three years."
149	          />
150	          <RowCard
151	            left="Premise 2"
152	            right="Nearby branches with longer hours serve more students after school."
153	          />
154	        </div>
155	      </LessonSection>
156	
157	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Student takeaway" className="space-y-8 border-b-0">
158	        <SectionHeading title="Student takeaway" subtitle="Your anatomy checkpoint" />
159	        <CalloutCard className="border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.92)] bg-[#1a1a1a] text-[#F5F2ED]">
160	          <p className="text-[22px] leading-[1.8]">
161	            First decide whether the stimulus is an argument at all. If it is, separate the supporting reasons from the claim they are trying to prove.
162	          </p>
163	        </CalloutCard>
164	      </LessonSection>
165	    </IntroNotesShell>
166	  );
167	}

77	      >
78	        <Kicker>The anchors</Kicker>
79	        <h2 className="font-mc-serif text-[40px] leading-tight italic mb-12">What must stay the same.</h2>
80	        
81	        <div className="grid grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 gap-4">

86	              </div>
87	              <div>
88	                <h3 className="font-mc-serif text-xl italic mb-2">Type</h3>
89	                <p className="font-mc-sans text-[15px] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.6)] leading-relaxed">
90	                  Prediction, judgment, recommendation, causal claim, or comparison. The flavor of the claim cannot change.
91	                </p>
92	              </div>
93	            </div>
94	          </div>
95	          <div className="p-8 bg-[#FAF7EF]/40 border border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.08)]">
96	            <div className="flex items-start gap-4">
97	              <div className="mt-1 h-5 w-5 rounded-full bg-[#1a1a1a] flex items-center justify-center">
98	                <Check className="h-3 w-3 text-white" strokeWidth={3} />
99	              </div>
100	              <div>
101	                <h3 className="font-mc-serif text-xl italic mb-2">Force</h3>
102	                <p className="font-mc-sans text-[15px] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.6)] leading-relaxed">
103	                  Absolute stays absolute. Hedged stays hedged. Do not turn "some" into "all" or "will" into "might."
104	                </p>
105	              </div>
106	            </div>
107	          </div>
108	          <div className="p-8 bg-[#FAF7EF]/40 border border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.08)]">
109	            <div className="flex items-start gap-4">
110	              <div className="mt-1 h-5 w-5 rounded-full bg-[#1a1a1a] flex items-center justify-center">
111	                <Check className="h-3 w-3 text-white" strokeWidth={3} />
112	              </div>
113	              <div>
114	                <h3 className="font-mc-serif text-xl italic mb-2">Scope</h3>
115	                <p className="font-mc-sans text-[15px] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.6)] leading-relaxed">
116	                  Do not change the group, amount, or category. If the author is talking about cats, the answer is not about all mammals.
117	                </p>
118	              </div>

121	          <div className="p-8 bg-[#FAF7EF]/40 border border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.08)]">

124	                <Check className="h-3 w-3 text-white" strokeWidth={3} />
125	              </div>
126	              <div>
127	                <h3 className="font-mc-serif text-xl italic mb-2">Direction</h3>
128	                <p className="font-mc-sans text-[15px] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.6)] leading-relaxed">
129	                  Do not reverse who affects whom or what supports what. Preserve the internal logic of the relationship.
130	                </p>

133	          </div>
134	        </div>
135	      </section>
136	
137	      {/* 4. Trap Patterns Section */}
138	      <section 
139	        data-toc

141	        className="p-12 desktop:p-16 border-b border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.1)] bg-[#FAF7EF]/20"
142	      >
143	        <Kicker>Trap Patterns</Kicker>
144	        <h2 className="font-mc-serif text-3xl italic mb-8">Common trap patterns</h2>
145	        
146	        <div className="space-y-3">
147	          {[
148	            { label: "Too strong", text: "The answer choice is more absolute than the author's actual claim." },
149	            { label: "Reverse", text: "The answer flips the premise and conclusion or a causal relationship." },
150	            { label: "Tense shift", text: "The answer moves from the past to the future or vice versa without reason." },
151	            { label: "Partial list", text: "The answer captures only one part of a multi-part conclusion." }
152	          ].map((trap) => (

159	                <span className="font-mc-sans text-[16px] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.7)]">{trap.text}</span>
160	              </div>

163	        </div>
164	      </section>

./content/lessons-phased.source.ts:163:      'Before we hunt the conclusion, we have to know what kind of beast we are hunting.',
./content/lessons-phased.source.ts:169:          'An argument is the author taking a position and giving you reasons to believe it. The reasons are premises. The position is the conclusion. Today you will spot the difference inside a real (silly) example.',
./content/lessons-phased.source.ts:321:            'A claim that goes beyond the premises. The author bets you will let it through. Push on it here.',

401	        <LessonSection tocTitle="The Centerpiece" className="space-y-8">
402	          <SectionHeading title="The Centerpiece" subtitle="What an argument actually is" />
403	          <p className="font-serif text-[30px] italic leading-[1.35] text-[#1a1a1a]">
404	            In Logical Reasoning, arguments are the single most important concept you need to understand on a deep level.
405	            We're going to learn to manipulate them, dismantle them, and beat them. But first, you need a crystal clear
406	            picture of what an argument actually is.
407	          </p>

412	          <p className="text-[20px] leading-[1.75] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.84)]">
413	            Arguments are quite simple at their core. An argument is made up of two things: premises and conclusions.
414	          </p>
415	          <QuoteCard className="bg-[#FBF8F1] pr-6">
416	            <div className="space-y-5">

418	                <p className="font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-[#1a1a1a]">Premises support conclusions.</p>
419	                <p>Premises are the evidence.</p>
420	              </div>

422	                <p className="font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-[#1a1a1a]">Conclusions rely on premises.</p>
423	                <p>Conclusions are the author's claim.</p>
424	              </div>
425	            </div>
426	          </QuoteCard>
427	          <p className="text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
428	            <strong>That's it.</strong> Arguments are just premises and conclusions. They have a lot of complicated
429	            baggage we'll introduce soon, but at their core, arguments are always this simple.
430	          </p>
431	        </LessonSection>

442	            </p>

499	                <p>Premises do the argument's heavy lifting. They're the evidence that makes the conclusion worth anything.</p>

626	          
627	          <div className="space-y-6 text-[18px] leading-[1.7] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.82)]">
628	            <p>Here's the thing, though. Indicator words are a tool, not a crutch.</p>
629	            
630	            <QuoteCard className="bg-[#FBF8F1] pr-6">
631	              <p>
632	                <strong>The goal is to change the way you think about sentences, not just tag them.</strong>
633	              </p>
634	              <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.7)]">They won't always be there. If your entire strategy is seek-and-find, you'll be stuck.</p>
635	            </QuoteCard>
636	
637	            <p>
638	              So go deeper. Ask yourself about the personality of each statement. Is it a fact, or is it a claim? Is it
639	              something being offered as evidence, or something being argued for? Facts are premises. Claims are
640	              conclusions.
641	            </p>
642	            <p>
643	              That instinct, reading the role of a sentence rather than hunting for a keyword, is what separates students
644	              who understand arguments from students who just sort of navigate them.
645	            </p>

667	            <p>Indicator words are packaging. They can point you toward a conclusion, but they do not tell you what kind of conclusion you are dealing with.</p>
668	            <p className="text-ink-bright font-semibold">That is the real question:</p>
669	            <p className="font-serif text-[28px] italic text-ink-bright leading-[1.3]">What kind of claim is the author making?</p>
670	            <p>
671	              This matters because conclusions are not all built the same way. Each type has its own shape and its own predictable weakness.
672	            </p>
673	            <p className="text-ink-bright font-semibold">
674	              So once you can name the type of conclusion, you are not just "finding the conclusion" anymore. You are already starting to see where the argument might break.

A <span className="font-sans text-[11px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.1em] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.4)] px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-sm bg-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.04)] mx-1">Concession</span> is a point the author is already giving away. An <span className="font-sans text-[11px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.1em] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.4)] px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded-sm bg-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.04)] mx-1">Opinion</span> might belong to a critic, not the author.

905	        </div>
906	      </LessonSection>
907	
908	      <LessonSection tocTitle="The Check" className="space-y-16">
909	        <SectionHeading title="The Check" subtitle="Run these two questions on every candidate" />
910	
911	        <ContentVignette variant="tool" accentColor="gold" className="shadow-[0_24px_64px_-12px_oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.08)]">
912	          <h3 className="text-xl font-bold text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)] mb-8 tracking-tight">The 2-Part Check</h3>
913	          <div className="grid gap-10">
914	              <div className="space-y-3">
915	                <p className="text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.4)]">Question 1</p>
916	                <p className="text-[20px] leading-tight text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)]"><strong className="font-bold">Is this the author's own opinion?</strong></p>
917	                <p className="text-[16px] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.64)] italic">In other words, is this a claim someone could reasonably disagree with?</p>
918	              </div>

922	                <p className="text-[20px] leading-tight text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)]"><strong className="font-bold">Is this claim supported by at least one other explicit statement?</strong></p>
923	                <p className="text-[16px] text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.64)] italic">In other words, does something else in the argument give us a reason to believe it?</p>
924	              </div>
925	          </div>
926	        </ContentVignette>
927	
928	        <div className="space-y-6 text-body-prose text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.76)] max-w-[64ch]">
929	          <p>Both answers must be <strong className="text-mc-success font-bold underline decoration-mc-success/20 underline-offset-4">yes</strong>.</p>
930	          <p>If either answer is <strong className="text-mc-error font-bold underline decoration-mc-error/20 underline-offset-4">no</strong>, that sentence is not the main conclusion.</p>
931	          <p>A factual statement fails the first question—facts are not the author's opinion. An unsupported claim fails the second question. A concession fails the first question.</p>
932	          <p className="font-bold text-[oklch(0.15_0.01_60)] pt-4 text-[18px]">Two questions. Both must be yes. No exceptions.</p>
933	        </div>
934	      </LessonSection>
935	
936	      <LessonSection tocTitle="Why This Works" className="space-y-16 border-b-0">
937	        <SectionHeading title="Why This Works" subtitle="Turn the definition into a check" />
938	        <div className="space-y-6 text-body-prose text-[oklch(0.2_0.01_60/0.76)] max-w-[64ch]">
939	          <p>A conclusion is the claim the author is trying to prove. This test turns that definition into two simple yes-or-no questions.</p>
940	          <p>If a sentence passes both, it is probably the conclusion. If it fails either, it is doing some other job—background, premise, opposing view, concession, or setup. Keep looking.</p>
941	        </div>
942	        <TwoPartConclusionCheckMachine />
943	      </LessonSection>
944	    </>

949	export function IntroLessonPage({ pageId }: { pageId: IntroLessonPageId }) {

952	  return (
953	    <IntroNotesShell eyebrow={page.eyebrow} title={page.title} subtitle={page.subtitle}>
954	      {page.content}
955	    </IntroNotesShell>
956	  );
957	}

const premiseIndicatorLabItems: PremiseIndicatorLabItem[] = [
  {
    id: "for",
    label: "For",
    chipNote: "Evidence",
    functionLabel: "Support follows the claim",
    teachingNote: `"For" commonly introduces a reason or evidence that supports the claim before it. Read what comes after "for" as the support.`,
    example: {
      conclusion: "You should try the curry,",
      indicator: "for",
      premise: "it is the restaurant's signature dish.",
      order: "conclusion-first",
    },
  },
  {
    id: "after-all",
    label: "After all",
    chipNote: "Support",
    functionLabel: "Reason arrives after the claim",
    teachingNote: `"After all" introduces a supporting reason after the claim—it's another way the author gives evidence.`,
    example: {
      conclusion: "You should try the curry.",
      indicator: "After all,",
      premise: "you like spicy food.",
      order: "conclusion-first",
    },
  },
  {
    id: "because",
    label: "Because",
    chipNote: "Reason",
    functionLabel: "Reason is introduced directly",
    teachingNote: `"Because" signals that what follows is the reason or explanation supporting the claim.`,
    example: {
      conclusion: "You should try the curry",
      indicator: "because",
      premise: "you like spicy food.",
      order: "conclusion-first",
    },
  },
  {
    id: "since",
    label: "Since",
    chipNote: "Fact",
    functionLabel: "Premise often appears first",
    teachingNote: `"Since" frequently puts the supporting fact or background first; the following text is usually the premise.`,
    example: {
      conclusion: "you should try the curry.",
      indicator: "Since",
      premise: "you like spicy food,",
      order: "premise-first",
    },
  },
];

export function PremiseIndicatorLab() {
  const [pinnedId, setPinnedId] = useState<string | null>(premiseIndicatorLabItems[0]?.id ?? null);
  const [hoveredId, setHoveredId] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [responses, setResponses] = useState<Record<string, PremiseIndicatorLabOption | null>>({});
  const reducedMotion = useReducedMotion();

  const activeId = hoveredId ?? pinnedId ?? premiseIndicatorLabItems[0]?.id ?? null;
  const activeItem = premiseIndicatorLabItems.find((item) => item.id === activeId) ?? premiseIndicatorLabItems[0];
  const selectedAnswer = activeItem ? responses[activeItem.id] ?? null : null;
  const hasAnswered = Boolean(selectedAnswer);
  const isCorrect = selectedAnswer === "premise";

  function setPinned(itemId: string) {
    setPinnedId((current) => (current === itemId ? null : itemId));
  }

  function setResponse(itemId: string, answer: PremiseIndicatorLabOption) {
    setResponses((current) => ({ ...current, [itemId]: answer }));
  }

  return (
    <InteractiveCard
      prominent
      className="overflow-hidden rounded-[26px] border-[#DFC28D] bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,#FFFEFA_0%,#FCF5E8_100%)] p-5 shadow-[0_24px_50px_rgba(92,67,20,0.08)] md:p-7"
    >
======
    );
  }

  return (
    <InteractiveCard prominent className="overflow-hidden border-[rgba(26,26,26,0.14)] bg-[linear-gradient(180deg,#fffdf9_0%,#fbf7ef_100%)] p-6 md:p-8">
      <div className="space-y-6">
        <div className="max-w-3xl space-y-3">
          <h3 className="font-serif text-[34px] italic leading-tight text-[#1a1a1a]">A conclusion needs support</h3>
          <p className="text-[17px] leading-[1.65] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.76)]">
            A conclusion is the claim the argument is trying to hold up.
          </p>
          <p className="text-[17px] leading-[1.65] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.76)]">
            Premises are the supports underneath it. They keep the claim standing.
          </p>
        </div>
            </motion.div>
          ) : null}
        </AnimatePresence>

        <p className="font-mc-serif text-[18px] italic leading-[1.65] text-[rgba(26,26,26,0.88)]">
          That is the basic structure of an argument: Premises support a conclusion.
        </p>
      </div>
  premise: string;
  conclusion: string;
};

const conclusionIndicatorItems: ConclusionIndicatorItem[] = [
  {
    id: "therefore",

    functionLabel: "Formal conclusion marker",
    teachingNote: `"Therefore" signals the author's main claim, drawn from the reasons just given.`,

    conclusion: "the city should repair the bridge.",
  },
  {
    id: "thus",

  },
  {
    id: "so",

    premise: "It has been raining all morning,",
    conclusion: "you should bring an umbrella.",

    conclusion: "the experiment's results cannot be trusted.",

];

import { sufficientConditionFilms } from '../content/sufficientConditionFilms';

export type SufficientConditionFilmAsset = { id: string; title: string; src: string; poster?: string; captions: string; caption: string; transcript: string };

/** Render only an actual generated asset; the written lesson remains available without video. */
export function SufficientConditionFilm({ assets = sufficientConditionFilms, title = 'What the rule gives us', ariaLabel = 'Short explanations of sufficiency' }: { assets?: SufficientConditionFilmAsset[]; title?: string; ariaLabel?: string }) {
  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);
  const [failedId, setFailedId] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0);
  const videoId = useId();
  const asset = assets[selected];
  if (!asset) return null;
  const failed = failedId === asset.id;
  return <section className="sc-film" aria-label={ariaLabel}>
    <header><p className="sc-eyebrow">Watch the explanation</p><h3>{title}</h3></header>
    {assets.length > 1 && <div className="sc-film-selector" role="group" aria-label="Choose an explanation">
      {assets.map((clip, index) => <button type="button" key={clip.id}
        aria-pressed={index === selected} aria-controls={videoId}
        onClick={() => { setSelected(index); setFailedId(null); }}>{clip.title}</button>)}
    </div>}
    <figure>
      <video id={videoId} key={`${asset.id}-${attempt}`} controls preload="metadata" playsInline
        poster={asset.poster} aria-label={asset.caption} onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}>
        <source src={asset.src} type="video/mp4" onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}/>
        <track kind="captions" src={asset.captions} srcLang="en" label="English" default/>
        Your browser cannot play this clip. The explanation is available below.
      </video>
      <figcaption>{asset.caption}</figcaption>
    </figure>
    {failed && <div className="sc-film-error">

import { useId, useState } from 'react';

const rooms = [
  { film: true, workshop: false, label: 'Film showing. No workshop.' },
  { film: false, workshop: false, label: 'No film. No workshop.' },
  { film: true, workshop: true, label: 'Film showing. Workshop underway.' },
];

export function NegativeCaseLab() {
  const [selected, setSelected] = useState<number[]>([]);
  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);
  const [workshopAnswer, setWorkshopAnswer] = useState<boolean | null>(null);
  const id = useId();
  const correct = selected.length === 2 && selected.includes(0) && selected.includes(1);
  return <div className="co-negative-lab">
    <p>Let's take the same reasoning into a sentence whose required condition contains a negative. Accept this rule for the exercise:</p>
    <blockquote>If a room is showing a film, it is not hosting a workshop.</blockquote>
    <p>Use <strong>F</strong> for showing a film and <strong>W</strong> for hosting a workshop. Our rule is <strong>F → ~W</strong>. A film requires the absence of a workshop. We haven't said a film is the only reason a room might have no workshop.</p>
    <div className="co-given-fact"><span className="co-kicker">Given fact</span><strong>This room is not hosting a workshop: ~W.</strong></div>
    <h3 id={id}>Which possible rooms fit both the rule and that fact? Select every one that fits.</h3>
    <div className="co-room-options" role="group" aria-labelledby={id}>{rooms.map((room, index) => <button type="button" key={room.label} aria-pressed={selected.includes(index)} onClick={() => { setSelected(items => items.includes(index) ? items.filter(i => i !== index) : [...items, index]); setChecked(false); }}><span className="co-room-drawing" aria-hidden="true"><i data-lit={room.film}/><b data
    <button type="button" className="co-primary" disabled={selected.length === 0} onClick={() => setChecked(true)}>Check these possibilities</button>
    <div role="status">{checked && <div className="co-feedback"><strong>{correct ? 'Both film possibilities remain open.' : selected.includes(2) ? 'A workshop conflicts with the given fact.' : 'There is another case that fits.'}</strong><p>{selected.includes(2) ? "The fact says no workshop. A film and a workshop would also break the original rule. Remove that room, then check the others." : "A fil
    {checked && correct && <div className="co-case-takeaway"><h3>Change the fact: now a workshop is underway.</h3><p>The rule stays F → ~W. Could this room also be showing a film?</p><div className="co-choices"><button type="button" aria-pressed={workshopAnswer === true} onClick={() => setWorkshopAnswer(true)}>Yes, it could.</button><button type="button" aria-pressed={workshopAnswer === false} o
  </div>;
}

const comparisons = [
  { sentence: 'Only stamped envelopes go into the dispatch tray.', same: true, reason: 'Only makes a stamp necessary for an envelope in the tray. An envelope in the tray must be stamped. A stamped envelope can still be elsewhere.' },
  { sentence: 'An envelope goes into the dispatch tray if it is stamped.', same: false, reason: 'If now makes the stamp sufficient for going into the tray. This sends every stamped envelope there. The original rule allowed stamped envelopes elsewhere.' },
  { sentence: 'The only envelopes in the dispatch tray are stamped envelopes.', same: true, reason: 'The only refers to envelopes in the tray. Being in the tray is enough to prove a stamp, just as in the original rule.' },
  { sentence: 'An envelope goes into the dispatch tray only if it is stamped.', same: true, reason: 'Only if makes being stamped a requirement for going into the tray. It does not promise a place in the tray to every stamped envelope.' },
];

export function RuleComparisonLab() {
  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);
  const [answers, setAnswers] = useState<Record<number, boolean>>({});
  const [explain, setExplain] = useState(false);
  const question = useId();
  const item = comparisons[index];
  const answer = answers[index];
  return <div className="co-comparison-lab">
    <p>You now know both groups. Set the group names aside and read what each rule promises.</p>
    <p className="co-kicker">Keep this original rule</p><blockquote>Every envelope in the dispatch tray is stamped.</blockquote>
    <p>An envelope in the tray has to be stamped. A stamped envelope could still be elsewhere. Use that possible case to check each new version.</p>
    <div className="co-word-tabs" role="group" aria-label="Wording variations">{comparisons.map((_, i) => <button type="button" key={i} aria-pressed={index === i} onClick={() => { setIndex(i); setExplain(false); }}>Version {i + 1}</button>)}</div>
    <blockquote>{item.sentence}</blockquote><h3 id={question}>Does this keep the original rule, or reverse it?</h3>
    <div className="co-choices" role="group" aria-labelledby={question}><button type="button" aria-pressed={answer === true} onClick={() => setAnswers({ ...answers, [index]: true })}>Same rule</button><button type="button" aria-pressed={answer === false} onClick={() => setAnswers({ ...answers, [index]: false })}>Reversed rule</button></div>
    <div role="status">{answer !== undefined && <div className="co-feedback"><strong>{answer === item.same ? 'The meaning matches your choice.' : 'Read the starting condition again.'}</strong><p>{item.reason}</p></div>}</div>
    {Object.keys(answers).length === comparisons.length && <div className="co-case-takeaway"><h3>One word can change what follows</h3><p>Compare versions 2 and 4. Before opening the explanation, say what adding only did to the role of the stamp.</p><button type="button" aria-expanded={explain} onClick={() => setExplain(!explain)}>Compare if with only if</button>{explain && <p>If makes the stamp en
  </div>;
}

// Original application of the Demon clips' concrete-to-abstract and case-testing approach.
export function CounterexampleLab() {
  const [drawer, setDrawer] = useState<boolean | null>(null);
  const [border, setBorder] = useState<boolean | null>(null);
  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);
  const [found, setFound] = useState(false);
  const id = useId();
  const fits = !drawer || border;
  const refutes = drawer === false && border === true;
  function change(which: 'drawer' | 'border', value: boolean) { (which === 'drawer' ? setDrawer : setBorder)(value); setChecked(false); }
  return <div className="co-case-maker">
    <p>So far, we've explained why an arrow cannot simply be reversed. Now make that limit visible.</p>
    <blockquote>Every ticket in Drawer R has a blue border.</blockquote>
    <p>Accept that rule for this exercise. We don't need to know why the tickets were arranged that way. A ticket in Drawer R must have a blue border.</p>
    <h3 id={id}>Someone claims: every blue-bordered ticket is in Drawer R. Can you build one ticket that fits the rule but disproves that claim?</h3>
    <div className="co-case-workbench">
      <div className="co-ticket" data-blue={border === true} aria-label={`Your ticket: ${drawer === null ? 'location undecided' : drawer ? 'in Drawer R' : 'outside Drawer R'}; ${border === null ? 'border undecided' : border ? 'blue border' : 'no blue border'}`}><span>ADMIT ONE</span><strong>R / 017</strong><span>{drawer === null ? 'Choose a location' : drawer ? 'In Drawer R' : 'Outside Drawer R'}<
      <div className="co-case-decisions" aria-describedby={id}>
        <fieldset><legend>Where is the ticket?</legend><button type="button" aria-pressed={drawer === true} onClick={() => change('drawer', true)}>In Drawer R</button><button type="button" aria-pressed={drawer === false} onClick={() => change('drawer', false)}>Outside Drawer R</button></fieldset>
        <fieldset><legend>What border does it have?</legend><button type="button" aria-pressed={border === true} onClick={() => change('border', true)}>Blue border</button><button type="button" aria-pressed={border === false} onClick={() => change('border', false)}>No blue border</button></fieldset>
      </div>
    </div>
    <button type="button" className="co-primary" disabled={drawer === null || border === null} onClick={() => { setChecked(true); if (refutes) setFound(true); }}>Test this ticket</button>
    <div role="status">{checked && <div className="co-feedback"><strong>{!fits ? 'This ticket breaks the original rule.' : refutes ? 'This fits the rule and disproves the reverse.' : 'This fits the rule. Does it disprove the claim?'}</strong><p>{!fits ? "A ticket in Drawer R has to have a blue border. We need a case the rule allows, so this cannot serve as our example." : refutes ? "The ticket has
    {found && <div className="co-case-takeaway"><h3>Now explain the difference</h3><p>The rule limits tickets <strong>inside the drawer</strong>. It doesn't put every ticket with a blue border there. A possible case is enough to show that the reverse doesn't follow; we're not claiming that this ticket actually exists.</p><p>Without looking back, say what changed when you reversed the rule.</p></di
  </div>;
}

import { useId, useRef, useState, type KeyboardEvent } from 'react';
import './necessary-indicator-explorer.css';

const examples = [
  { expression: 'Only', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote><strong>Only</strong> students are enrolled in law school.</blockquote><p>This excludes nonstudents from the law-school group. It does not put every student in that group. Being a student remains required; being enrolled remains enough to prove it.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Only if', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>You are enrolled in law school <strong>only if</strong> you are a student.</blockquote><p>Only if introduces the requirement: <strong>you are a student</strong>. Read the whole phrase. The if inside only if does not make being a student sufficient.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Only when', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>You are enrolled in law school <strong>only when</strong> you are a student.</blockquote><p>On any occasion when enrollment is true, being a student must be true too. This does not say you become enrolled whenever you are a student, or that one condition has to happen later than the other.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Only where', necessaryCondition: 'A tutor is present where the seminar takes place.', content: <><p>This indicator restricts places, so use a setting example:</p><blockquote>A seminar takes place <strong>only where</strong> a tutor is present.</blockquote><p>At any place with that seminar, <strong>a tutor must be present</strong>. A tutor could also be somewhere without a seminar.
  { expression: 'Always', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>While you are enrolled in law school, you are <strong>always</strong> a student.</blockquote><p>Always allows no exception within the situation described: being enrolled in law school. It does not say every student is enrolled, or that someone remains a student after enrollment ends.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Requires', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>Being enrolled in law school <strong>requires</strong> being a student.</blockquote><p>What does enrollment require? <strong>Being a student.</strong> Requires is the indicator; being a student is the condition it identifies. They are not the same thing.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Must', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>If you are enrolled in law school, you <strong>must</strong> be a student.</blockquote><p>Must makes the certainty explicit: enrollment cannot coexist with not being a student. Here it describes what has to be true, not a task someone has been told to complete.</p></> },
];

export function NecessaryIndicatorExplorer() {
  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);
  const tabs = useRef<(HTMLButtonElement | null)[]>([]);
  const id = useId();
  function navigate(event: KeyboardEvent<HTMLButtonElement>, index: number) {
    let next: number;
    switch (event.key) {
      case 'ArrowDown': case 'ArrowRight': next = (index + 1) % examples.length; break;
      case 'ArrowUp': case 'ArrowLeft': next = (index + examples.length - 1) % examples.length; break;
      case 'Home': next = 0; break;
      case 'End': next = examples.length - 1; break;
      default: return;
    }
    event.preventDefault();
    setSelected(next);
    tabs.current[next]?.focus();
  }
  return <div className="ni-explorer">
    <p className="ni-instruction">Choose an indicator to see the necessary condition it identifies in a complete sentence.</p>
    <div className="ni-layout">
      <ol className="ni-expression-list" role="tablist" aria-label="Necessary-condition indicators" aria-orientation="vertical">
        {examples.map((example, index) => <li key={example.expression} role="presentation">
          <button type="button" role="tab" id={`${id}-tab-${index}`} aria-selected={selected === index}
            tabIndex={selected === index ? 0 : -1} aria-controls={`${id}-example-${index}`}
            ref={element => { tabs.current[index] = element; }} onKeyDown={event => navigate(event, index)}
            onPointerEnter={event => {
              if (event.pointerType === 'mouse' && window.matchMedia('(hover: hover) and (min-width: 621px)').matches) setSelected(index);
            }}
            onFocus={() => setSelected(index)} onClick={() => setSelected(index)}>
            <span className="ni-number" aria-hidden="true">{String(index + 1).padStart(2, '0')}</span>
            <span>{example.expression.toLowerCase()}</span><span className="ni-indicator" aria-hidden="true">↗</span>
          </button>
        </li>)}
      </ol>
      <div className="ni-examples">
        {examples.map((example, index) => <section key={example.expression} id={`${id}-example-${index}`}
          role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby={`${id}-tab-${index}`} tabIndex={0} hidden={selected !== index} className="ni-example">
          <p className="ni-example-label">{String(index + 1).padStart(2, '0')} / {String(examples.length).padStart(2, '0')} · The rule and its meaning</p>
          <dl className="nc-indicator-examples"><div><dt>{example.expression}</dt><dd>{example.content}</dd></div></dl>
          <div className="ni-requirement"><span>Necessary condition</span><strong>{example.necessaryCondition}</strong></div>
        </section>)}
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>;
}

#root .nc-lesson .ni-explorer { margin-block: 1.75rem 3rem; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-instruction { font-size: 1.05rem; color: var(--sc-muted); margin-bottom: 1.2rem; }
#root .ni-layout { display: grid; grid-template-columns: minmax(9rem, .65fr) minmax(0, 2fr); gap: clamp(1rem, 3vw, 2rem); align-items: start; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-expression-list { list-style: none; margin: 0; padding: 0; display: grid; gap: .25rem; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-expression-list li { margin: 0; padding: 0!important; border: 0; }
#root .ni-expression-list button { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 1.6rem 1fr 1rem; gap: .5rem; align-items: center; width: 100%; min-height: 48px; margin: 0; padding: .6rem .75rem; text-align: left; border: 1px solid transparent; border-radius: .6rem; background: transparent; color: var(--sc-
#root .ni-expression-list button[aria-selected="true"] { background: var(--sc-ink); color: var(--sc-white); box-shadow: 0 5px 12px -7px var(--sc-ink); }
@media (hover: hover) { #root .ni-expression-list button:hover { transform: translateX(3px); } }
#root .ni-expression-list button:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--sc-ink); outline-offset: 3px; }
#root .ni-number { font-size: .75rem; font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; opacity: .72; }
#root .ni-expression-list button > span:nth-child(2) { white-space: nowrap; overflow-wrap: normal; word-break: normal; }
#root .ni-indicator { opacity: 0; }
#root .ni-expression-list button[aria-selected="true"] .ni-indicator { opacity: 1; }
#root .ni-examples { min-width: 0; }
#root .ni-example { padding: clamp(1.1rem, 3vw, 2rem); background: linear-gradient(135deg, var(--sc-white), oklch(96% .018 82)); border: 1px solid var(--sc-line); border-radius: 1rem; box-shadow: 0 14px 30px -24px var(--sc-ink); }
#root .ni-example:not([hidden]) { animation: ni-enter 140ms ease-out; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-example-label { font-size: .8rem; color: var(--sc-muted); margin: 0 0 1rem; letter-spacing: .025em; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-example .nc-indicator-examples { margin: 0; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-example .nc-indicator-examples > div { padding: 0; border: 0; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-example .nc-indicator-examples dt { font-family: Georgia, serif; font-size: 2rem; font-weight: 400; margin-bottom: 1.1rem; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-example blockquote { font-size: clamp(1.25rem, 2.5vw, 1.6rem); line-height: 1.6; padding: 0 0 0 1rem; border: 0; border-left: 3px solid var(--sc-brass); background: transparent; margin: 0 0 1.25rem; }
#root .nc-lesson .ni-example p { font-size: 1.1rem; line-height: 1.7; }
#root .ni-requirement { border-top: 1px solid var(--sc-line); margin-top: 1.5rem; padding-top: 1rem; display: grid; gap: .45rem; }
#root .ni-requirement span { font-size: .85rem; color: var(--sc-muted); }
#root .ni-requirement strong { font-size: 1.2rem; color: var(--sc-ink); }
@keyframes ni-enter { from { transform: translateY(3px); } to { transform: translateY(0); } }
@media (max-width: 620px) {
  #root .ni-layout { grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr); }
  #root .nc-lesson .ni-expression-list { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr)); gap: .45rem; }
  #root .ni-expression-list button { font-size: 1rem; border-color: var(--sc-line); padding: .7rem .6rem; }
}
@media (max-width: 380px) {
  #root .nc-lesson .ni-expression-list { grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr); }
}
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  #root .ni-expression-list button { transition: none; }
  #root .ni-expression-list button:hover { transform: none; }
  #root .ni-example:not([hidden]) { animation: none; }
}

/**

 * drill actually asks for, in the same order, using an example sentence that
 * is not one of the drill's own questions.
